then | indeed | Bhikkhave | of the group of five | bhikkhū | by me | thus | being instructed | thus | being admonished | by themselves | liable to birth | being | in liable to birth | unsatisfactoriness | having perceived | unborn | unsurpassed | sanctuary | extinguishment | searching for | unborn | unsurpassed | sanctuary | extinguishment | achieved | by themselves | liable to old age | being | in liable to old age | unsatisfactoriness | having perceived | not ageing | unsurpassed | sanctuary | extinguishment | searching for | not ageing | unsurpassed | sanctuary | extinguishment | achieved | by themselves | liable to get sick | being | (and so on) | by themselves | liable to death | being | by themselves | liable to sorrow | being | by themselves | liable to defilement | being | in liable to defilement | unsatisfactoriness | having perceived | undefiled | unsurpassed | sanctuary | extinguishment | searching for | undefiled | unsurpassed | sanctuary | extinguishment | achieved  
Then, Bhikkhave, the group of five bhikkhū, being thus instructed and thus admonished by me, from their own doing were liable to birth, and having perceived the unsatisfactoriness of being liable to birth, searching for the unsurpassed sanctuary of extinguishment from being born, achieved the unsurpassed sanctuary of extinguishment from being born. From their own doing they were liable to old age, and having perceived the unsatisfactoriness of being liable to old age, searching for the unsurpassed sanctuary of non-ageing through extinguishment, achieved the unsurpassed sanctuary of non-ageing through extinguishment. From their own doing they were liable to sickness, and having perceived the unsatisfactoriness of being liable to sickness, searching for the unsurpassed sanctuary of health through extinguishment, achieved the unsurpassed sanctuary of health through extinguishment. From their own doing they were liable to death, and having perceived the unsatisfactoriness of being liable to death, searching for the unsurpassed sanctuary of non-death through extinguishment, achieved the unsurpassed sanctuary of non-death through extinguishment. From their own doing they were liable to sorrow, and having perceived the unsatisfactoriness of being liable to sorrow, searching for the unsurpassed sanctuary of non-sorrow through extinguishment, achieved the unsurpassed sanctuary of non-sorrow through extinguishment. From their own doing they were liable to defilement, and having perceived the unsatisfactoriness of being liable to defilement, searching for the unsurpassed sanctuary of undefilement through extinguishment, achieved the unsurpassed sanctuary of undefilement through extinguishment.

993.2 Ñāṇañca pana nesaṃ dassanaṃ udapādi—  ‘akuppā no vimutti, ayamantimā jāti, natthi dāni punabbhavo’ti.  
🚻⨀①(ñāṇa) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(pana) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂④(ta) 🚻⨀①(dassana) 🟢⨀🤟⏮(uppajjati) 🚺⨀①🆎(na+kuppa) *️⃣👆⚧⨂⑥(ahaṃ) 🚺⨀①(vimutti) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀①(ima) 🚺⨀①(antima) 🚺⨀①(jāti) ⏏️(na) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(atthi) ⏏️(dāni) 🚹⨀①🆎(puna+bhava) ⏏️(iti)  
knowledge | and | moreover | for them | insight | arose | unshakeable | our | liberation | this | final | birth | not | there is | now | appearing again | (end quote)  
And knowledge and insight arose in them: ‘Unshakeable is our liberation, this is the final birth (of suffering), there is now no more appearing again (of suffering).’

## Heap of Traps (discourse)

<Tabs syncKey="paliquote">
<TabItem label="My Translation">
994\. There are, Bhikkhave, these five `kāmaguṇā` (objects of sensual desire). What five? Forms cognisable by the eye — agreeable, pleasant, charming, alluring, accompanied by sensual desire, and stimulating; sounds cognisable by the ear—agreeable, pleasant, charming, alluring, accompanied by sensual desire, and stimulating; smells cognisable by the nose—agreeable, pleasant, charming, alluring, accompanied by sensual desire, and stimulating; tastes cognisable by the tongue—agreeable, pleasant, charming, alluring, accompanied by sensual desire, and stimulating; tactile objects cognisable by the body—agreeable, pleasant, charming, alluring, accompanied by sensual desire, and stimulating. These, Bhikkhave, are the five objects of sensual desire. Whatever wanderers or brahmins, Bhikkhave, enjoy these five objects of sensual desire, entangled, infatuated, and engrossed in them, not seeing the unsatisfactoriness in them and having no understanding of the escape from them, they should be known as being ‘fallen into disaster, fallen into calamity, subject to the Evil One’s will’.

995\. Just as, Bhikkhave, a wild deer, bound, might lie down on a heap of traps. It should be known as being ‘fallen into disaster, fallen into calamity, subject to the hunter’s will. And when the hunter comes, it will not be able to go wherever it wants.’ In the same way, Bhikkhave, whatever wanderers or brahmins enjoy these five objects of sensual desire, entangled, infatuated, and engrossed in them, not seeing the unsatisfactoriness in them and having no understanding of the escape from them, they should be known as being ‘fallen into disaster, fallen into calamity, subject to the Evil One’s will’. But whatever wanderers or brahmins, Bhikkhave, enjoy these five objects of sensual desire, not entangled, not infatuated, and not engrossed in them, seeing the unsatisfactoriness in them and having an understanding of the escape from them, they should be known as being ‘not fallen into disaster, not fallen into calamity, not subject to the Evil One’s will’.

996\. Just as, Bhikkhave, a wild deer, unbound, might lie down on a heap of traps. It should be known as being ‘not fallen into disaster, not fallen into calamity, not subject to the hunter’s will. And when the hunter comes, it will be able to go wherever it wants.’ In the same way, Bhikkhave, whatever wanderers or brahmins enjoy these five objects of sensual desire, not entangled, not infatuated, and not engrossed in them, seeing the unsatisfactoriness in them and having an understanding of the escape from them, they should be known as being ‘not fallen into disaster, not fallen into calamity, not subject to the Evil One’s will’.

997\. Just as, Bhikkhave, a wild deer roaming in the wilderness, goes confidently, stands confidently, sits confidently, sleeps confidently. For what reason? It is beyond the reach of the hunter, Bhikkhave. In the same way, Bhikkhave, a bhikkhu, secluding himself from sensual pleasures, detaching from unwholesome characteristics, enters and remains in the first `jhāna` (mental disposition), accompanied by reflection and consideration, secluded from defilements, with felicity and satisfaction. This, Bhikkhave, is called a bhikkhu who has blinded Māra, made him footless, and having struck out Māra’s eye, has gone beyond the sight of the Evil One.

998\. And furthermore, Bhikkhave, a bhikkhu, with the conclusion of reflection and consideration, enters and remains in the second mental disposition, accompanied by an inner calming of intention, focused, without reflection, without consideration, born from focus, with felicity and satisfaction. This, Bhikkhave, is called a bhikkhu who has blinded Māra, made him footless, and having struck out Māra’s eye, has gone beyond the sight of the Evil One.

999\. And furthermore, Bhikkhave, a bhikkhu, with the detachment from felicity, remains equanimous, aware and clearly comprehending, and experiences body comfort, as the liberated ones describe: ‘equanimous, aware, living at ease’ — he enters and remains in the third mental disposition. This, Bhikkhave, is called a bhikkhu who has blinded Māra, made him footless, and having struck out Māra’s eye, has gone beyond the sight of the Evil One.

1000\. And furthermore, Bhikkhave, a bhikkhu, with the giving up of the pleasant and the unpleasant, and with the previous disappearance of mental pleasure and displeasure, enters and remains in the fourth mental disposition, in a neutral state, and possessing purification of awareness by equanimity. This, Bhikkhave, is called a bhikkhu who has blinded Māra, made him footless, and having struck out Māra’s eye, has gone beyond the sight of the Evil One.

1001\. And furthermore, Bhikkhave, a bhikkhu, going beyond `rūpasaññā` (apperceptions of form) altogether, with the disappearance of apperceptions of sense impressions, ignoring the apperceptions of diversity, thinking: ‘Space is infinite,’ enters and remains in the base of the infinity of space. This, Bhikkhave, is called a bhikkhu who has blinded Māra, made him footless, and having struck out Māra’s eye, has gone beyond the sight of the Evil One.

1002\. And furthermore, Bhikkhave, a bhikkhu, going beyond the base of the infinity of space altogether, thinking: ‘Consciousness is infinite,’ enters and remains in the base of the infinity of consciousness. This, Bhikkhave, is called a bhikkhu who has blinded Māra, made him footless, and having struck out Māra’s eye, has gone beyond the sight of the Evil One.

1003\. And furthermore, Bhikkhave, a bhikkhu, going beyond the base of the infinity of consciousness altogether, thinking: ‘There is nothing,’ enters and remains in the base of nothingness. This, Bhikkhave, is called a bhikkhu who has blinded Māra, made him footless, and having struck out Māra’s eye, has gone beyond the sight of the Evil One.

1004\. And furthermore, Bhikkhave, a bhikkhu, going beyond the base of nothingness altogether, enters and remains in the base of neither apperception nor non-apperception. This, Bhikkhave, is called a bhikkhu who has blinded Māra, made him footless, and having struck out Māra’s eye, has gone beyond the sight of the Evil One.

1005\. And furthermore, Bhikkhave, a bhikkhu, going beyond the base of neither apperception nor non-apperception altogether, enters and remains in the cessation of apperception and feeling. And having seen with wisdom, his defilements are destroyed. This, Bhikkhave, is called a bhikkhu who has blinded Māra, made him footless, and having struck out Māra’s eye, has gone beyond the sight of the Evil One. Having crossed over the entanglement in the world, he goes confidently, stands confidently, sits confidently, sleeps confidently. For what reason? He is beyond the reach of the Evil One, Bhikkhave.”

1006\. This the Bhagavā said. Those bhikkhū, delighted, rejoiced in the Bhagavā’s words.

1007\. The Discourse on the Heap of Traps, the sixth, is finished.
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Roman IAST)">
994\. Pañcime, bhikkhave, kāmaguṇā. Katame pañca? Cakkhuviññeyyā rūpā iṭṭhā kantā manāpā piyarūpā kāmūpasaṃhitā rajanīyā, sotaviññeyyā saddā…pe…  ghānaviññeyyā gandhā…  jivhāviññeyyā rasā…  kāyaviññeyyā phoṭṭhabbā iṭṭhā kantā manāpā piyarūpā kāmūpasaṃhitā rajanīyā. Ime kho, bhikkhave, pañca kāmaguṇā. Ye hi keci, bhikkhave, samaṇā vā brāhmaṇā vā ime pañca kāmaguṇe gathitā mucchitā ajjhopannā anādīnavadassāvino anissaraṇapaññā paribhuñjanti, te evamassu veditabbā—  ‘anayamāpannā byasanamāpannā yathākāmakaraṇīyā pāpimato’.

995\. Seyyathāpi, bhikkhave, āraññako mago baddho pāsarāsiṃ adhisayeyya. So evamassa veditabbo—  ‘anayamāpanno byasanamāpanno yathākāmakaraṇīyo luddassa. Āgacchante ca pana ludde yena kāmaṃ na pakkamissatī’ti. Evameva kho, bhikkhave, ye hi keci samaṇā vā brāhmaṇā vā ime pañca kāmaguṇe gathitā mucchitā ajjhopannā anādīnavadassāvino anissaraṇapaññā paribhuñjanti, te evamassu veditabbā—  ‘anayamāpannā byasanamāpannā yathākāmakaraṇīyā pāpimato’. Ye ca kho keci, bhikkhave, samaṇā vā brāhmaṇā vā ime pañca kāmaguṇe agathitā amucchitā anajjhopannā ādīnavadassāvino nissaraṇapaññā paribhuñjanti, te evamassu veditabbā—  ‘na anayamāpannā na byasanamāpannā na yathākāmakaraṇīyā pāpimato’.

996\. Seyyathāpi, bhikkhave, āraññako mago abaddho pāsarāsiṃ adhisayeyya. So evamassa veditabbo—  ‘na anayamāpanno na byasanamāpanno na yathākāmakaraṇīyo luddassa. Āgacchante ca pana ludde yena kāmaṃ pakkamissatī’ti. Evameva kho, bhikkhave, ye hi keci samaṇā vā brāhmaṇā vā ime pañca kāmaguṇe agathitā amucchitā anajjhopannā ādīnavadassāvino nissaraṇapaññā paribhuñjanti, te evamassu veditabbā—  ‘na anayamāpannā na byasanamāpannā na yathākāmakaraṇīyā pāpimato’.

997\. Seyyathāpi, bhikkhave, āraññako mago araññe pavane caramāno vissattho gacchati, vissattho tiṭṭhati, vissattho nisīdati, vissattho seyyaṃ kappeti. Taṃ kissa hetu? Anāpāthagato, bhikkhave, luddassa. Evameva kho, bhikkhave, bhikkhu vivicceva kāmehi vivicca akusalehi dhammehi savitakkaṃ savicāraṃ vivekajaṃ pītisukhaṃ paṭhamaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave, bhikkhu andhamakāsi māraṃ apadaṃ, vadhitvā māracakkhuṃ adassanaṃ gato pāpimato.

998\. Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu vitakkavicārānaṃ vūpasamā ajjhattaṃ sampasādanaṃ cetaso ekodibhāvaṃ avitakkaṃ avicāraṃ samādhijaṃ pītisukhaṃ dutiyaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave…pe…  pāpimato.

999\. Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu pītiyā ca virāgā upekkhako ca viharati, sato ca sampajāno, sukhañca kāyena paṭisaṃvedeti yaṃ taṃ ariyā ācikkhanti ‘upekkhako satimā sukhavihārī’ti tatiyaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave…pe…  pāpimato.

1000\. Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu sukhassa ca pahānā dukkhassa ca pahānā pubbeva somanassadomanassānaṃ atthaṅgamā adukkhamasukhaṃ upekkhāsatipārisuddhiṃ catutthaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave…pe…  pāpimato.

1001\. Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu sabbaso rūpasaññānaṃ samatikkamā paṭighasaññānaṃ atthaṅgamā nānattasaññānaṃ amanasikārā ‘ananto ākāso’ti ākāsānañcāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave…pe…  pāpimato.

1002\. Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu sabbaso ākāsānañcāyatanaṃ samatikkamma ‘anantaṃ viññāṇan’ti viññāṇañcāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave…pe…  pāpimato.

1003\. Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu sabbaso viññāṇañcāyatanaṃ samatikkamma ‘natthi kiñcī’ti ākiñcaññāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave…pe…  pāpimato.

1004\. Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu sabbaso ākiñcaññāyatanaṃ samatikkamma nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave…pe…  pāpimato.

1005\. Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu sabbaso nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ samatikkamma saññāvedayitanirodhaṃ upasampajja viharati, paññāya cassa disvā āsavā parikkhīṇā honti. Ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave, bhikkhu andhamakāsi māraṃ apadaṃ, vadhitvā māracakkhuṃ adassanaṃ gato pāpimato. Tiṇṇo loke visattikaṃ vissattho gacchati, vissattho tiṭṭhati, vissattho nisīdati, vissattho seyyaṃ kappeti. Taṃ kissa hetu? Anāpāthagato, bhikkhave, pāpimato”ti.

1006\. Idamavoca bhagavā. Attamanā te bhikkhū bhagavato bhāsitaṃ abhinandunti.

1007\. Pāsarāsisuttaṃ niṭṭhitaṃ chaṭṭhaṃ.
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Brahmi)">
994\. 𑀧𑀜𑁆𑀘𑀺𑀫𑁂, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀓𑀸𑀫𑀕𑀼𑀡𑀸. 𑀓𑀢𑀫𑁂 𑀧𑀜𑁆𑀘? 𑀘𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀼𑀯𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀸 𑀭𑀽𑀧𑀸 𑀇𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀸 𑀓𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀸 𑀫𑀦𑀸𑀧𑀸 𑀧𑀺𑀬𑀭𑀽𑀧𑀸 𑀓𑀸𑀫𑀽𑀧𑀲𑀁𑀳𑀺𑀢𑀸 𑀭𑀚𑀦𑀻𑀬𑀸, 𑀲𑁄𑀢𑀯𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀸 𑀲𑀤𑁆𑀤𑀸…𑀧𑁂…  𑀖𑀸𑀦𑀯𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀸 𑀕𑀦𑁆𑀥𑀸…  𑀚𑀺𑀯𑁆𑀳𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀸 𑀭𑀲𑀸…  𑀓𑀸𑀬𑀯𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀸 𑀨𑁄𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸 𑀇𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀸 𑀓𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀸 𑀫𑀦𑀸𑀧𑀸 𑀧𑀺𑀬𑀭𑀽𑀧𑀸 𑀓𑀸𑀫𑀽𑀧𑀲𑀁𑀳𑀺𑀢𑀸 𑀭𑀚𑀦𑀻𑀬𑀸. 𑀇𑀫𑁂 𑀔𑁄, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀧𑀜𑁆𑀘 𑀓𑀸𑀫𑀕𑀼𑀡𑀸. 𑀬𑁂 𑀳𑀺 𑀓𑁂𑀘𑀺, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀲𑀫𑀡𑀸 𑀯𑀸 𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀸𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀡𑀸 𑀯𑀸 𑀇𑀫𑁂 𑀧𑀜𑁆𑀘 𑀓𑀸𑀫𑀕𑀼𑀡𑁂 𑀕𑀣𑀺𑀢𑀸 𑀫𑀼𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀢𑀸 𑀅𑀚𑁆𑀛𑁄𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸 𑀅𑀦𑀸𑀤𑀻𑀦𑀯𑀤𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀦𑁄 𑀅𑀦𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀭𑀡𑀧𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀪𑀼𑀜𑁆𑀚𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺, 𑀢𑁂 𑀏𑀯𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀼 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀺𑀢𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸—  ‘𑀅𑀦𑀬𑀫𑀸𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸 𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀲𑀦𑀫𑀸𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸 𑀬𑀣𑀸𑀓𑀸𑀫𑀓𑀭𑀡𑀻𑀬𑀸 𑀧𑀸𑀧𑀺𑀫𑀢𑁄’.

995\. 𑀲𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀣𑀸𑀧𑀺, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀆𑀭𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀓𑁄 𑀫𑀕𑁄 𑀩𑀤𑁆𑀥𑁄 𑀧𑀸𑀲𑀭𑀸𑀲𑀺𑀁 𑀅𑀥𑀺𑀲𑀬𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬. 𑀲𑁄 𑀏𑀯𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀺𑀢𑀩𑁆𑀩𑁄—  ‘𑀅𑀦𑀬𑀫𑀸𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑁄 𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀲𑀦𑀫𑀸𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑁄 𑀬𑀣𑀸𑀓𑀸𑀫𑀓𑀭𑀡𑀻𑀬𑁄 𑀮𑀼𑀤𑁆𑀤𑀲𑁆𑀲. 𑀆𑀕𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁂 𑀘 𑀧𑀦 𑀮𑀼𑀤𑁆𑀤𑁂 𑀬𑁂𑀦 𑀓𑀸𑀫𑀁 𑀦 𑀧𑀓𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀢𑀻’𑀢𑀺. 𑀏𑀯𑀫𑁂𑀯 𑀔𑁄, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀬𑁂 𑀳𑀺 𑀓𑁂𑀘𑀺 𑀲𑀫𑀡𑀸 𑀯𑀸 𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀸𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀡𑀸 𑀯𑀸 𑀇𑀫𑁂 𑀧𑀜𑁆𑀘 𑀓𑀸𑀫𑀕𑀼𑀡𑁂 𑀕𑀣𑀺𑀢𑀸 𑀫𑀼𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀢𑀸 𑀅𑀚𑁆𑀛𑁄𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸 𑀅𑀦𑀸𑀤𑀻𑀦𑀯𑀤𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀦𑁄 𑀅𑀦𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀭𑀡𑀧𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀪𑀼𑀜𑁆𑀚𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺, 𑀢𑁂 𑀏𑀯𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀼 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀺𑀢𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸—  ‘𑀅𑀦𑀬𑀫𑀸𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸 𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀲𑀦𑀫𑀸𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸 𑀬𑀣𑀸𑀓𑀸𑀫𑀓𑀭𑀡𑀻𑀬𑀸 𑀧𑀸𑀧𑀺𑀫𑀢𑁄’. 𑀬𑁂 𑀘 𑀔𑁄 𑀓𑁂𑀘𑀺, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀲𑀫𑀡𑀸 𑀯𑀸 𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀸𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀡𑀸 𑀯𑀸 𑀇𑀫𑁂 𑀧𑀜𑁆𑀘 𑀓𑀸𑀫𑀕𑀼𑀡𑁂 𑀅𑀕𑀣𑀺𑀢𑀸 𑀅𑀫𑀼𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀢𑀸 𑀅𑀦𑀚𑁆𑀛𑁄𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸 𑀆𑀤𑀻𑀦𑀯𑀤𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀦𑁄 𑀦𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀭𑀡𑀧𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀪𑀼𑀜𑁆𑀚𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺, 𑀢𑁂 𑀏𑀯𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀼 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀺𑀢𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸—  ‘𑀦 𑀅𑀦𑀬𑀫𑀸𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸 𑀦 𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀲𑀦𑀫𑀸𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸 𑀦 𑀬𑀣𑀸𑀓𑀸𑀫𑀓𑀭𑀡𑀻𑀬𑀸 𑀧𑀸𑀧𑀺𑀫𑀢𑁄’.

996\. 𑀲𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀣𑀸𑀧𑀺, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀆𑀭𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀓𑁄 𑀫𑀕𑁄 𑀅𑀩𑀤𑁆𑀥𑁄 𑀧𑀸𑀲𑀭𑀸𑀲𑀺𑀁 𑀅𑀥𑀺𑀲𑀬𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬. 𑀲𑁄 𑀏𑀯𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀺𑀢𑀩𑁆𑀩𑁄—  ‘𑀦 𑀅𑀦𑀬𑀫𑀸𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑁄 𑀦 𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀲𑀦𑀫𑀸𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑁄 𑀦 𑀬𑀣𑀸𑀓𑀸𑀫𑀓𑀭𑀡𑀻𑀬𑁄 𑀮𑀼𑀤𑁆𑀤𑀲𑁆𑀲. 𑀆𑀕𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁂 𑀘 𑀧𑀦 𑀮𑀼𑀤𑁆𑀤𑁂 𑀬𑁂𑀦 𑀓𑀸𑀫𑀁 𑀧𑀓𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀢𑀻’𑀢𑀺. 𑀏𑀯𑀫𑁂𑀯 𑀔𑁄, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀬𑁂 𑀳𑀺 𑀓𑁂𑀘𑀺 𑀲𑀫𑀡𑀸 𑀯𑀸 𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀸𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀡𑀸 𑀯𑀸 𑀇𑀫𑁂 𑀧𑀜𑁆𑀘 𑀓𑀸𑀫𑀕𑀼𑀡𑁂 𑀅𑀕𑀣𑀺𑀢𑀸 𑀅𑀫𑀼𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀢𑀸 𑀅𑀦𑀚𑁆𑀛𑁄𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸 𑀆𑀤𑀻𑀦𑀯𑀤𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀦𑁄 𑀦𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀭𑀡𑀧𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀪𑀼𑀜𑁆𑀚𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺, 𑀢𑁂 𑀏𑀯𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀼 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀺𑀢𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸—  ‘𑀦 𑀅𑀦𑀬𑀫𑀸𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸 𑀦 𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀲𑀦𑀫𑀸𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸 𑀦 𑀬𑀣𑀸𑀓𑀸𑀫𑀓𑀭𑀡𑀻𑀬𑀸 𑀧𑀸𑀧𑀺𑀫𑀢𑁄’.

997\. 𑀲𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀣𑀸𑀧𑀺, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀆𑀭𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀓𑁄 𑀫𑀕𑁄 𑀅𑀭𑀜𑁆𑀜𑁂 𑀧𑀯𑀦𑁂 𑀘𑀭𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀯𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀢𑁆𑀣𑁄 𑀕𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀢𑀺, 𑀯𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀢𑁆𑀣𑁄 𑀢𑀺𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀢𑀺, 𑀯𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀢𑁆𑀣𑁄 𑀦𑀺𑀲𑀻𑀤𑀢𑀺, 𑀯𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀢𑁆𑀣𑁄 𑀲𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀁 𑀓𑀧𑁆𑀧𑁂𑀢𑀺. 𑀢𑀁 𑀓𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀳𑁂𑀢𑀼? 𑀅𑀦𑀸𑀧𑀸𑀣𑀕𑀢𑁄, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀮𑀼𑀤𑁆𑀤𑀲𑁆𑀲. 𑀏𑀯𑀫𑁂𑀯 𑀔𑁄, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀼 𑀯𑀺𑀯𑀺𑀘𑁆𑀘𑁂𑀯 𑀓𑀸𑀫𑁂𑀳𑀺 𑀯𑀺𑀯𑀺𑀘𑁆𑀘 𑀅𑀓𑀼𑀲𑀮𑁂𑀳𑀺 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁂𑀳𑀺 𑀲𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀓𑁆𑀓𑀁 𑀲𑀯𑀺𑀘𑀸𑀭𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀓𑀚𑀁 𑀧𑀻𑀢𑀺𑀲𑀼𑀔𑀁 𑀧𑀞𑀫𑀁 𑀛𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀢𑀺. 𑀅𑀬𑀁 𑀯𑀼𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀢𑀺, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀼 𑀅𑀦𑁆𑀥𑀫𑀓𑀸𑀲𑀺 𑀫𑀸𑀭𑀁 𑀅𑀧𑀤𑀁, 𑀯𑀥𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀫𑀸𑀭𑀘𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀼𑀁 𑀅𑀤𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦𑀁 𑀕𑀢𑁄 𑀧𑀸𑀧𑀺𑀫𑀢𑁄.

998\. 𑀧𑀼𑀦 𑀘𑀧𑀭𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀼 𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀓𑁆𑀓𑀯𑀺𑀘𑀸𑀭𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀯𑀽𑀧𑀲𑀫𑀸 𑀅𑀚𑁆𑀛𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀲𑀸𑀤𑀦𑀁 𑀘𑁂𑀢𑀲𑁄 𑀏𑀓𑁄𑀤𑀺𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀁 𑀅𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀓𑁆𑀓𑀁 𑀅𑀯𑀺𑀘𑀸𑀭𑀁 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀥𑀺𑀚𑀁 𑀧𑀻𑀢𑀺𑀲𑀼𑀔𑀁 𑀤𑀼𑀢𑀺𑀬𑀁 𑀛𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀢𑀺. 𑀅𑀬𑀁 𑀯𑀼𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀢𑀺, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂…𑀧𑁂…  𑀧𑀸𑀧𑀺𑀫𑀢𑁄.

999\. 𑀧𑀼𑀦 𑀘𑀧𑀭𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀼 𑀧𑀻𑀢𑀺𑀬𑀸 𑀘 𑀯𑀺𑀭𑀸𑀕𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑁂𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀓𑁄 𑀘 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀢𑀺, 𑀲𑀢𑁄 𑀘 𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑀸𑀦𑁄, 𑀲𑀼𑀔𑀜𑁆𑀘 𑀓𑀸𑀬𑁂𑀦 𑀧𑀝𑀺𑀲𑀁𑀯𑁂𑀤𑁂𑀢𑀺 𑀬𑀁 𑀢𑀁 𑀅𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸 𑀆𑀘𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺 ‘𑀉𑀧𑁂𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀓𑁄 𑀲𑀢𑀺𑀫𑀸 𑀲𑀼𑀔𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀸𑀭𑀻’𑀢𑀺 𑀢𑀢𑀺𑀬𑀁 𑀛𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀢𑀺. 𑀅𑀬𑀁 𑀯𑀼𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀢𑀺, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂…𑀧𑁂…  𑀧𑀸𑀧𑀺𑀫𑀢𑁄.

1000\. 𑀧𑀼𑀦 𑀘𑀧𑀭𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀼 𑀲𑀼𑀔𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀘 𑀧𑀳𑀸𑀦𑀸 𑀤𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀘 𑀧𑀳𑀸𑀦𑀸 𑀧𑀼𑀩𑁆𑀩𑁂𑀯 𑀲𑁄𑀫𑀦𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀤𑁄𑀫𑀦𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀗𑁆𑀕𑀫𑀸 𑀅𑀤𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀫𑀲𑀼𑀔𑀁 𑀉𑀧𑁂𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀸𑀲𑀢𑀺𑀧𑀸𑀭𑀺𑀲𑀼𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀺𑀁 𑀘𑀢𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀁 𑀛𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀢𑀺. 𑀅𑀬𑀁 𑀯𑀼𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀢𑀺, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂…𑀧𑁂…  𑀧𑀸𑀧𑀺𑀫𑀢𑁄.

1001\. 𑀧𑀼𑀦 𑀘𑀧𑀭𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀼 𑀲𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀲𑁄 𑀭𑀽𑀧𑀲𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀲𑀫𑀢𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀸 𑀧𑀝𑀺𑀖𑀲𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀗𑁆𑀕𑀫𑀸 𑀦𑀸𑀦𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀲𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀅𑀫𑀦𑀲𑀺𑀓𑀸𑀭𑀸 ‘𑀅𑀦𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁄 𑀆𑀓𑀸𑀲𑁄’𑀢𑀺 𑀆𑀓𑀸𑀲𑀸𑀦𑀜𑁆𑀘𑀸𑀬𑀢𑀦𑀁 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀢𑀺. 𑀅𑀬𑀁 𑀯𑀼𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀢𑀺, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂…𑀧𑁂…  𑀧𑀸𑀧𑀺𑀫𑀢𑁄.

1002\. 𑀧𑀼𑀦 𑀘𑀧𑀭𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀼 𑀲𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀲𑁄 𑀆𑀓𑀸𑀲𑀸𑀦𑀜𑁆𑀘𑀸𑀬𑀢𑀦𑀁 𑀲𑀫𑀢𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀓𑀫𑁆𑀫 ‘𑀅𑀦𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀡𑀦𑁆’𑀢𑀺 𑀯𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀡𑀜𑁆𑀘𑀸𑀬𑀢𑀦𑀁 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀢𑀺. 𑀅𑀬𑀁 𑀯𑀼𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀢𑀺, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂…𑀧𑁂…  𑀧𑀸𑀧𑀺𑀫𑀢𑁄.

1003\. 𑀧𑀼𑀦 𑀘𑀧𑀭𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀼 𑀲𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀲𑁄 𑀯𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀡𑀜𑁆𑀘𑀸𑀬𑀢𑀦𑀁 𑀲𑀫𑀢𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀓𑀫𑁆𑀫 ‘𑀦𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀓𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀘𑀻’𑀢𑀺 𑀆𑀓𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀘𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀬𑀢𑀦𑀁 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀢𑀺. 𑀅𑀬𑀁 𑀯𑀼𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀢𑀺, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂…𑀧𑁂…  𑀧𑀸𑀧𑀺𑀫𑀢𑁄.

1004\. 𑀧𑀼𑀦 𑀘𑀧𑀭𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀼 𑀲𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀲𑁄 𑀆𑀓𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀘𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀬𑀢𑀦𑀁 𑀲𑀫𑀢𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀓𑀫𑁆𑀫 𑀦𑁂𑀯𑀲𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀲𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀬𑀢𑀦𑀁 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀢𑀺. 𑀅𑀬𑀁 𑀯𑀼𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀢𑀺, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂…𑀧𑁂…  𑀧𑀸𑀧𑀺𑀫𑀢𑁄.

1005\. 𑀧𑀼𑀦 𑀘𑀧𑀭𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀼 𑀲𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀲𑁄 𑀦𑁂𑀯𑀲𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀲𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀬𑀢𑀦𑀁 𑀲𑀫𑀢𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀓𑀫𑁆𑀫 𑀲𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀬𑀺𑀢𑀦𑀺𑀭𑁄𑀥𑀁 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀢𑀺, 𑀧𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀬 𑀘𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀤𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀆𑀲𑀯𑀸 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀻𑀡𑀸 𑀳𑁄𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺. 𑀅𑀬𑀁 𑀯𑀼𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀢𑀺, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀼 𑀅𑀦𑁆𑀥𑀫𑀓𑀸𑀲𑀺 𑀫𑀸𑀭𑀁 𑀅𑀧𑀤𑀁, 𑀯𑀥𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀫𑀸𑀭𑀘𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀼𑀁 𑀅𑀤𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦𑀁 𑀕𑀢𑁄 𑀧𑀸𑀧𑀺𑀫𑀢𑁄. 𑀢𑀺𑀡𑁆𑀡𑁄 𑀮𑁄𑀓𑁂 𑀯𑀺𑀲𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀺𑀓𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀢𑁆𑀣𑁄 𑀕𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀢𑀺, 𑀯𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀢𑁆𑀣𑁄 𑀢𑀺𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀢𑀺, 𑀯𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀢𑁆𑀣𑁄 𑀦𑀺𑀲𑀻𑀤𑀢𑀺, 𑀯𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀢𑁆𑀣𑁄 𑀲𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀁 𑀓𑀧𑁆𑀧𑁂𑀢𑀺. 𑀢𑀁 𑀓𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀳𑁂𑀢𑀼? 𑀅𑀦𑀸𑀧𑀸𑀣𑀕𑀢𑁄, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀧𑀸𑀧𑀺𑀫𑀢𑁄”𑀢𑀺.

1006\. 𑀇𑀤𑀫𑀯𑁄𑀘 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀸. 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀫𑀦𑀸 𑀢𑁂 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀽 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀢𑁄 𑀪𑀸𑀲𑀺𑀢𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀦𑀦𑁆𑀤𑀼𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺.

1007\. 𑀧𑀸𑀲𑀭𑀸𑀲𑀺𑀲𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀺𑀢𑀁 𑀙𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀁.

994.1 Pañcime, bhikkhave, kāmaguṇā.  
⚧⨂①(pañca) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(ima) 🚹⨂⓪(bhikkhu) 🚹⨂①🆎(kāma+guṇa)  
five | these | Bhikkhave | objects of sensual desire  
There are, Bhikkhave, these five `kāmaguṇā` (objects of sensual desire).

994.2 Katame pañca?  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(katama) ⚧⨂①(pañca)  
which | five  
What five?

994.3 Cakkhuviññeyyā rūpā iṭṭhā kantā manāpā piyarūpā kāmūpasaṃhitā rajanīyā, sotaviññeyyā saddā…pe…  ghānaviññeyyā gandhā…  jivhāviññeyyā rasā…  kāyaviññeyyā phoṭṭhabbā iṭṭhā kantā manāpā piyarūpā kāmūpasaṃhitā rajanīyā.  
🚻⨂①🆎(cakkhu+viññeyya) 🚻⨂①(rūpa) 🚻⨂①🔽(ta)(iṭṭha) 🚻⨂①🔽(ta)(kanta) 🚻⨂①(manāpa) 🚻⨂①🆎(piya+rūpa) 🚻⨂①🆎(kāma+upasaṃhita) 🚻⨂①🔽(anīya)(rajanīya) 🚹⨂①🆎(sota+viññeyya) 🚹⨂①(sadda) ⏏️(peyyāla) 🚹⨂①🆎(ghāna+viññeyya) 🚹⨂①(gandha) 🚹⨂①🆎(jivhā+viññeyya) 🚹⨂①(rasa) 🚻⨂①🆎(kāya+viññeyya) 🚻⨂①(phoṭṭhabba) 🚻⨂①🔽(ta)(iṭṭha) 🚻⨂①🔽(ta)(kanta) 🚻⨂①(manāpa) 🚻⨂①🆎(piya+rūpa) 🚻⨂①🆎(kāma+upasaṃhita) 🚻⨂①🔽(anīya)(rajanīya)  
cognisable by the eye | forms | agreeable | pleasant | charming | alluring | accompanied by sensual desire | stimulating | cognisable by the ear | sounds | (and so on) | cognisable by the nose | odours | cognisable by the tongue | tastes | cognisable by the body | tangibles | agreeable | pleasant | charming | alluring | accompanied by sensual desire | stimulating  
Forms cognisable by the eye — agreeable, pleasant, charming, alluring, accompanied by sensual desire, and stimulating; sounds cognisable by the ear—agreeable, pleasant, charming, alluring, accompanied by sensual desire, and stimulating; smells cognisable by the nose—agreeable, pleasant, charming, alluring, accompanied by sensual desire, and stimulating; tastes cognisable by the tongue—agreeable, pleasant, charming, alluring, accompanied by sensual desire, and stimulating; tactile objects cognisable by the body—agreeable, pleasant, charming, alluring, accompanied by sensual desire, and stimulating.

994.4 Ime kho, bhikkhave, pañca kāmaguṇā.  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(ima) ⏏️(kho) 🚹⨂⓪(bhikkhu) ⚧⨂①(pañca) 🚹⨂①🆎(kāma+guṇa)  
these | indeed | Bhikkhave | five | objects of sensual desire  
These, Bhikkhave, are the five objects of sensual desire.

994.5 Ye hi keci, bhikkhave, samaṇā vā brāhmaṇā vā ime pañca kāmaguṇe gathitā mucchitā ajjhopannā anādīnavadassāvino anissaraṇapaññā paribhuñjanti, te evamassu veditabbā—  ‘anayamāpannā byasanamāpannā yathākāmakaraṇīyā pāpimato’.  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(ya) ⏏️(hi) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(kaci) 🚹⨂⓪(bhikkhu) 🚹⨂①(samaṇa) ⏏️(vā) 🚹⨂①(brāhmaṇa) ⏏️(vā) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂②(ima) ⚧⨂②(pañca) 🚹⨂②🆎(kāma+guṇa) 🚹⨂①🔽(ta)(gathita) 🚹⨂①🔽(ta)(mucchita) 🚹⨂①🔽(ta)(ajjhopanna) 🚹⨂①🆎(na+ādīnava+dassāvī) 🚹⨂①🆎(na+nissaraṇa+pañña) 🟢⨂🤟▶️(paribhuñjati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(ta) ⏏️(evaṃ) 🟢⨂🤟⏯(atthi) 🚹⨂①🔽(tabba)(veditabba) 🚹⨂①🆎(anaya+āpanna) 🚹⨂①🆎(byasana+āpanna) 🚹⨂①🆎(yathā+kāma+karaṇīya) 🚹⨀⑥(pāpimant)  
whatever | indeed | any | Bhikkhave | wanderers | or | brahmins | or | these | five | objects of sensual desire | entangled | infatuated | engrossed | not seeing the unsatisfactoriness | having no understanding of the escape | enjoy | they | thus | should be | known | fallen into disaster | fallen into calamity | subject to the will | of the Evil One  
Whatever wanderers or brahmins, Bhikkhave, enjoy these five objects of sensual desire, entangled, infatuated, and engrossed in them, not seeing the unsatisfactoriness in them and having no understanding of the escape from them, they should be known as being ‘fallen into disaster, fallen into calamity, subject to the Evil One’s will’.

995.1 Seyyathāpi, bhikkhave, āraññako mago baddho pāsarāsiṃ adhisayeyya.  
⏏️(seyyathāpi) 🚹⨂⓪(bhikkhu) 🚹⨀①(āraññaka) 🚹⨀①(maga) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(baddha) 🚹⨀②🆎(pāsa+rāsi) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(adhisayati)  
just as | Bhikkhave | wild | deer | bound | on a heap of traps | might lie down  
Just as, Bhikkhave, a wild deer, bound, might lie down on a heap of traps.

995.2 So evamassa veditabbo—  ‘anayamāpanno byasanamāpanno yathākāmakaraṇīyo luddassa.  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(ta) ⏏️(evaṃ) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(atthi) 🚹⨀①🔽(tabba)(veditabba) 🚹⨀①🆎(anaya+āpanna) 🚹⨀①🆎(byasana+āpanna) 🚹⨀①🆎(yathā+kāma+karaṇīya) 🚹⨀⑥(ludda)  
it | thus | should be | known | fallen into disaster | fallen into calamity | subject to the will | of the hunter  
It should be known as being ‘fallen into disaster, fallen into calamity, subject to the hunter’s will.

995.3 Āgacchante ca pana ludde yena kāmaṃ na pakkamissatī’ti.  
🚹⨀⑦🔽(anta)(āgacchanta) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(pana) 🚹⨀⑦(ludda) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(ya) ⏏️(kāmaṃ) ⏏️(na) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(pakkamati) ⏏️(iti)  
coming | and | moreover | when the hunter | wherever | as it wants | not | will go away | (end quote)  
And when the hunter comes, it will not be able to go wherever it wants.’

995.4 Evameva kho, bhikkhave, ye hi keci samaṇā vā brāhmaṇā vā ime pañca kāmaguṇe gathitā mucchitā ajjhopannā anādīnavadassāvino anissaraṇapaññā paribhuñjanti, te evamassu veditabbā—  ‘anayamāpannā byasanamāpannā yathākāmakaraṇīyā pāpimato’.  
⏏️(evaṃ) ⏏️(eva) ⏏️(kho) 🚹⨂⓪(bhikkhu) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(ya) ⏏️(hi) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(kaci) 🚹⨂①(samaṇa) ⏏️(vā) 🚹⨂①(brāhmaṇa) ⏏️(vā) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂②(ima) ⚧⨂②(pañca) 🚹⨂②🆎(kāma+guṇa) 🚹⨂①🔽(ta)(gathita) 🚹⨂①🔽(ta)(mucchita) 🚹⨂①🔽(ta)(ajjhopanna) 🚹⨂①🆎(na+ādīnava+dassāvī) 🚹⨂①🆎(na+nissaraṇa+pañña) 🟢⨂🤟▶️(paribhuñjati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(ta) ⏏️(evaṃ) 🟢⨂🤟⏯(atthi) 🚹⨂①🔽(tabba)(veditabba) 🚹⨂①🆎(anaya+āpanna) 🚹⨂①🆎(byasana+āpanna) 🚹⨂①🆎(yathā+kāma+karaṇīya) 🚹⨀⑥(pāpimant)  
in the same way | just | indeed | Bhikkhave | whatever | indeed | any | wanderers | or | brahmins | or | these | five | objects of sensual desire | entangled | infatuated | engrossed | not seeing the unsatisfactoriness | having no understanding of the escape | enjoy | they | thus | should be | known | fallen into disaster | fallen into calamity | subject to the will | of the Evil One  
In the same way, Bhikkhave, whatever wanderers or brahmins enjoy these five objects of sensual desire, entangled, infatuated, and engrossed in them, not seeing the unsatisfactoriness in them and having no understanding of the escape from them, they should be known as being ‘fallen into disaster, fallen into calamity, subject to the Evil One’s will’.

995.5 Ye ca kho keci, bhikkhave, samaṇā vā brāhmaṇā vā ime pañca kāmaguṇe agathitā amucchitā anajjhopannā ādīnavadassāvino nissaraṇapaññā paribhuñjanti, te evamassu veditabbā—  ‘na anayamāpannā na byasanamāpannā na yathākāmakaraṇīyā pāpimato’.  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(ya) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(kho) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(kaci) 🚹⨂⓪(bhikkhu) 🚹⨂①(samaṇa) ⏏️(vā) 🚹⨂①(brāhmaṇa) ⏏️(vā) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂②(ima) ⚧⨂②(pañca) 🚹⨂②🆎(kāma+guṇa) 🚹⨂①🔽(ta)🆎(na+gathita) 🚹⨂①🔽(ta)🆎(na+mucchita) 🚹⨂①🔽(ta)🆎(na+ajjhopanna) 🚹⨂①🆎(ādīnava+dassāvī) 🚹⨂①🆎(nissaraṇa+pañña) 🟢⨂🤟▶️(paribhuñjati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(ta) ⏏️(evaṃ) 🟢⨂🤟⏯(atthi) 🚹⨂①🔽(tabba)(veditabba) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨂①🆎(anaya+āpanna) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨂①🆎(byasana+āpanna) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨂①🆎(yathā+kāma+karaṇīya) 🚹⨀⑥(pāpimant)  
whatever | but | indeed | any | Bhikkhave | wanderers | or | brahmins | or | these | five | objects of sensual desire | not entangled | not infatuated | not engrossed | seeing the unsatisfactoriness | having an understanding of the escape | enjoy | they | thus | should be | known | not | fallen into disaster | not | fallen into calamity | not | subject to the will | of the Evil One  
But whatever wanderers or brahmins, Bhikkhave, enjoy these five objects of sensual desire, not entangled, not infatuated, and not engrossed in them, seeing the unsatisfactoriness in them and having an understanding of the escape from them, they should be known as being ‘not fallen into disaster, not fallen into calamity, not subject to the Evil One’s will’.

996.1 Seyyathāpi, bhikkhave, āraññako mago abaddho pāsarāsiṃ adhisayeyya.  
⏏️(seyyathāpi) 🚹⨂⓪(bhikkhu) 🚹⨀①(āraññaka) 🚹⨀①(maga) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+baddha) 🚹⨀②🆎(pāsa+rāsi) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(adhisayati)  
just as | Bhikkhave | wild | deer | unbound | on a heap of traps | might lie down  
Just as, Bhikkhave, a wild deer, unbound, might lie down on a heap of traps.

996.2 So evamassa veditabbo—  ‘na anayamāpanno na byasanamāpanno na yathākāmakaraṇīyo luddassa.  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(ta) ⏏️(evaṃ) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(atthi) 🚹⨀①🔽(tabba)(veditabba) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①🆎(anaya+āpanna) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①🆎(byasana+āpanna) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①🆎(yathā+kāma+karaṇīya) 🚹⨀⑥(ludda)  
it | thus | should be | known | not | fallen into disaster | not | fallen into calamity | not | subject to the will | of the hunter  
It should be known as being ‘not fallen into disaster, not fallen into calamity, not subject to the hunter’s will.

996.3 Āgacchante ca pana ludde yena kāmaṃ pakkamissatī’ti.  
🚹⨀⑦🔽(anta)(āgacchanta) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(pana) 🚹⨀⑦(ludda) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(ya) ⏏️(kāmaṃ) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(pakkamati) ⏏️(iti)  
coming | and | moreover | when the hunter | wherever | as it wants | will go away | (end quote)  
And when the hunter comes, it will be able to go wherever it wants.’

996.4 Evameva kho, bhikkhave, ye hi keci samaṇā vā brāhmaṇā vā ime pañca kāmaguṇe agathitā amucchitā anajjhopannā ādīnavadassāvino nissaraṇapaññā paribhuñjanti, te evamassu veditabbā—  ‘na anayamāpannā na byasanamāpannā na yathākāmakaraṇīyā pāpimato’.

in the same way | just | indeed | Bhikkhave | whatever | indeed | any | wanderers | or | brahmins | or | these | five | objects of sensual desire | not entangled | not infatuated | not engrossed | seeing the unsatisfactoriness | having an understanding of the escape | enjoy | they | thus | should be | known | not | fallen into disaster | not | fallen into calamity | not | subject to the will | of the Evil One  
In the same way, Bhikkhave, whatever wanderers or brahmins enjoy these five objects of sensual desire, not entangled, not infatuated, and not engrossed in them, seeing the unsatisfactoriness in them and having an understanding of the escape from them, they should be known as being ‘not fallen into disaster, not fallen into calamity, not subject to the Evil One’s will’.

997.1 Seyyathāpi, bhikkhave, āraññako mago araññe pavane caramāno vissattho gacchati, vissattho tiṭṭhati, vissattho nisīdati, vissattho seyyaṃ kappeti.

Just as, Bhikkhave, a wild deer roaming in the wilderness, goes confidently, stands confidently, sits confidently, sleeps confidently.

997.2 Taṃ kissa hetu?

For what reason?

It is beyond the reach of the hunter, Bhikkhave.

In the same way, Bhikkhave, a bhikkhu, secluding himself from sensual pleasures, detaching from unwholesome characteristics, enters and remains in the first `jhāna` (mental disposition), accompanied by reflection and consideration, secluded from defilements, with felicity and satisfaction.

997.5 Ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave, bhikkhu andhamakāsi māraṃ apadaṃ, vadhitvā māracakkhuṃ adassanaṃ gato pāpimato.

998.1 Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu vitakkavicārānaṃ vūpasamā ajjhattaṃ sampasādanaṃ cetaso ekodibhāvaṃ avitakkaṃ avicāraṃ samādhijaṃ pītisukhaṃ dutiyaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati.

again | and | further | Bhikkhave | a bhikkhu | of felicity | and | with the detachment | equanimous | and | remains | aware | and | clearly comprehending | comfort | and | with the body | experiences | which | that | the liberated ones | describe | equanimous | aware | living at ease | (end quote) | third | mental disposition | entering | remains in  
And furthermore, Bhikkhave, a bhikkhu, with the detachment from felicity, remains equanimous, aware and clearly comprehending, and experiences body comfort, as the liberated ones describe: ‘equanimous, aware, living at ease’ — he enters and remains in the third mental disposition.

1000.1 Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu sukhassa ca pahānā dukkhassa ca pahānā pubbeva somanassadomanassānaṃ atthaṅgamā adukkhamasukhaṃ upekkhāsatipārisuddhiṃ catutthaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati.

again | and | further | Bhikkhave | a bhikkhu | of the pleasant | and | with the giving up | of the unpleasant | and | with the giving up | before | just | of mental pleasure and displeasure | with the disappearance | neutral | possessing purification of awareness by equanimity | fourth | mental disposition | entering | remains in  
And furthermore, Bhikkhave, a bhikkhu, with the giving up of the pleasant and the unpleasant, and with the previous disappearance of mental pleasure and displeasure, enters and remains in the fourth mental disposition, in a neutral state, and possessing purification of awareness by equanimity.

1001.1 Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu sabbaso rūpasaññānaṃ samatikkamā paṭighasaññānaṃ atthaṅgamā nānattasaññānaṃ amanasikārā ‘ananto ākāso’ti ākāsānañcāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharati.

And furthermore, Bhikkhave, a bhikkhu, going beyond `rūpasaññā` (apperceptions of form) altogether, with the disappearance of apperceptions of sense impressions, ignoring the apperceptions of diversity, thinking: ‘Space is infinite,’ enters and remains in the base of the infinity of space.

1004.1 Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu sabbaso ākiñcaññāyatanaṃ samatikkamma nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharati.

And furthermore, Bhikkhave, a bhikkhu, going beyond the base of neither apperception nor non-apperception altogether, enters and remains in the cessation of apperception and feeling. And having seen with wisdom, his defilements are destroyed.

This, Bhikkhave, is called a bhikkhu who has blinded Māra, made him footless, and having struck out Māra’s eye, has gone beyond the sight of the Evil One.

1005.3 Tiṇṇo loke visattikaṃ vissattho gacchati, vissattho tiṭṭhati, vissattho nisīdati, vissattho seyyaṃ kappeti.  
🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(tiṇṇa) 🚹⨀⑦(loka) 🚺⨀②(visattikā) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(vissattha) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(gacchati) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(vissattha) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(tiṭṭhati) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(vissattha) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(nisīdati) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(vissattha) 🚺⨀②(seyyā) 🟠⨀🤟▶️(kappeti)  
having crossed over | in the world | the entanglement | confidently | goes | confidently | stands | confidently | sits | confidently | lying down | makes  
Having crossed over the entanglement in the world, he goes confidently, stands confidently, sits confidently, sleeps confidently.

1005.4 Taṃ kissa hetu?  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(ta) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀⑥(kiṃ) 🚹⨀①(hetu)  
that | of what | the reason  
For what reason?

1005.5 Anāpāthagato, bhikkhave, pāpimato”ti.  
🚹⨀①🆎(na+āpātha+gata) 🚹⨂⓪(bhikkhu) 🚹⨀⑥(pāpimant) ⏏️(iti)  
beyond the reach | Bhikkhave | of the Evil One | (end quote)  
He is beyond the reach of the Evil One, Bhikkhave.”

1006.1 Idamavoca bhagavā.  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀②(ima) 🟢⨀🤟⏮(vadati) 🚹⨀①(bhagavant)  
this | said | the Bhagavā  
This the Bhagavā said.

1006.2 Attamanā te bhikkhū bhagavato bhāsitaṃ abhinandunti.  
🚹⨂①🆎(atta+mana) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(ta) 🚹⨂①(bhikkhu) 🚹⨀⑥(bhagavant) 🚻⨀②🔽(ta)(bhāsita) 🟢⨂🤟⏮(abhinandati) ⏏️(iti)  
delighted | those | bhikkhū | of the Bhagavā | what was spoken | rejoiced in | (end quote)  
Those bhikkhū, delighted, rejoiced in the Bhagavā’s words.

1007.1 Pāsarāsisuttaṃ niṭṭhitaṃ chaṭṭhaṃ.  
🚻⨀①🆎(pāsa+rāsi+sutta) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)(niṭṭhita) 🚻⨀①(chaṭṭha)  
the Pāsarāsisutta | finished | the sixth  
The Discourse on the Heap of Traps, the sixth, is finished.
